# Deferred items register
Everything found but not fixed: review minors that were deferred, and notes a later tracker row must act on. Nothing deferred lives only in the executor ledger, which is git-ignored and local to one machine.
**Rules**
1. When a review's minors are deferred, or a task finds something a later row must handle, the item is added here **in the same commit** as the work that found it.
2. Each item names a **target** tracker row. That row's Notes column lists its open items (`Deferred: DF-nn`).
3. The plan for a sub-plan pulls in every open item that targets its rows. The sub-plan's review checks they are closed.
4. An item is closed by a commit with a test, or by a recorded decision. Its status then becomes `Done (<commit>)`, `Accepted (<reason>)` or `Dropped` (dropped items also go in `docs/de-scope-register.md`).
5. `Needs decision` items are raised with the owner, never decided silently.
6. Items with no natural home go to **M1-81** (the hardening pass before the M1 demo).
**Status values:** `Open` · `Needs decision` · `Done (<commit>)` · `Accepted (<reason>)` · `Dropped`

There is no allow_values list or pragma yet. | M1-41 follow-up | Done (686245c: documented example keys and an allow-list are not secrets) |

Only regex detectors are available (IPPublicDetector is off by default). | new SP11 row (egress scanner coverage) | Open (retargeted by M2 review security: Entropy and keyword detectors in the egress scanner need line heuristics and a per-call performance budget; this is egress scanning, not the pre-pass. gitleaks (with entropy) covers review where it is installed.) |
| DF-153 | SP11/13 | Scanning runs on the event loop | Scanning a large request body (about 55 ms per 500 KB) blocks the asyncio loop briefly. It could move to a thread with regex concurrent=True. | M1-41 follow-up | Done (M1 close runtime: ffb4e30) |
| DF-154 | SP11/13 review | F3: secret-scan audit events cannot be tied to a run | The event carries only action, where and detector counts: no run_id, step, agent, team or policy_version, and a model call's 'where' is a constant. The fix is for NativeBackend to build a default scanner_for(step) that wraps on_event and adds the step's identity, for both the model-call and tool paths, so the M1-44 sink can store it. Record the Zyroflo extension-event decision in DEFERRED.md. | M1-44 | Done (M1 close runtime: ffb4e30) |
| DF-155 | SP11/13 review | F4: NpmDetector backtracks quadratically on long single-line text; the scan runs on the event loop | Minified bundles and one-line text full of URLs scan slowly: 3.6 s for 510 KB, and 1 MB hits the 10 s deadline, after which the text counts as Unscannable and is blocked or masked. The fix is to scan in bounded windows or cap match length, cache strings already found clean, run asyncio.to_thread, and add a test with a URL-heavy single line. (The new password rules are bounded and tested as linear; the history scrub now runs in pydantic-ai's executor thread and scans each message once.) | M1-41 follow-up | Done (M1 close runtime: ffb4e30) |
| DF-156 | SP11/13 review | F5: the host-path scrub also rewrites file content | read_file output that contains the tree's absolute path is shown with '.', so an edit whose old_string was copied from it fails with 'found 0'. The fix is to scrub only framework-written text (or run_shell, search and list_dir output) and leave read_file content verbatim, or to say in DATA_NOTICE that paths are shown relative. | M1-42 follow-up | Done (M1 close runtime: 0938065) |
| DF-157 | SP11/13 review | F6: a pywintypes.error from the Windows keyring escapes as a raw exception, not CredentialError | WinVaultKeyring re-raises a pywintypes.error that is not 'not found', and that class is not a KeyringError, OSError or RuntimeError. A locked credential store or an over-size secret therefore surfaces as a raw exception instead of the actionable CredentialError. The fix is to catch Exception around keyring calls, convert it to CredentialError naming only the type, and test it with a bare Exception subclass. | M1-48 follow-up / M1-53 | Done (SP14a 5fc86c8: keyring errors become CredentialError) |
| DF-158 | SP11/13 review | F7: CI=off counts as CI, and the CI error does not say which marker fired | A developer with a stray CI=off, or a leftover GITHUB_ACTIONS or JENKINS_URL, gets 'no key in this CI job' and the keyring is skipped silently. The fix is to add 'off' to the false values and name the marker that triggered it in the error. | M1-48 follow-up / M1-53 | Done (SP14a 5fc86c8: CI=off is not CI; the error names the marker) |

| DF-468 | M2 engine safety | POSIX: a hard kill (SIGKILL) of the CLI leaves its agent processes running | Windows covers this case through the job object. Possible fixes: PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER, cgroups, or heartbeat-based zombie cleanup. | M3-17 | Open (retargeted by M3-07: Heartbeat zombie detection now frees the control-plane slot and alerts when the CLI dies by SIGKILL. | DF-469 | M2 engine safety | POSIX: a grandchild that calls setsid and outlives its parent escapes every sweep | A double-forked daemon started outside the srt sandbox on macOS or Linux is re-parented to init and leaves no trace (not in the group, the session or the descendants). | DF-470 | M2 engine safety | Windows: SDK-started CLIs are adopted just after spawn, not from a suspended start | claude-code is adopted by a 10 ms poll and ACP right after create_subprocess_exec. | DF-471 | M2 engine safety | Codex: on a normal exit, shell commands it started in their own sessions are not swept by descendants | codex exec exits by itself, so its setsid'd commands are re-parented before tree.close runs. | DF-475 | M2 engine safety | Process-table scans at scope close are not batched | Each live tree's kill reads /proc twice. | DF-476 | M2 engine safety review | F3 residual: a reused pid whose new group leader has already exited can still be group-killed at scope close | POSIX only and needs a narrow race (our leftovers die, the pid is reused by a group leader that spawns a child and exits, all inside one step). | DF-477 | M2 engine safety review | F5: the review-batch line count skips added/removed lines starting with ++ or -- and counts empty lines; --text expands binaries | Content such as ++i; or -- sql comments is not counted against the line budget, and a large binary is read into memory. Count with git diff --numstat, or fix Zyroflo's header test (upstream). | M1-81 | Open (retargeted by M2-58: the review-batch line count, not the M2-58 variant removal.) |
| DF-478 | M2 engine safety review | F6: settle() and tree.kill() scan /proc (or run taskkill) synchronously on the event loop after each shell command | Stalls the loop for milliseconds per command on busy hosts. | DF-769 | M3 run health | A control-plane outage longer than stale_minutes marks live runs stale (then revives them) | If ingest is unreachable but the worker keeps running, live runs get a spurious run.stale mail and audit pair, then run.revived on the next beat. The slot is briefly freed, so a revived run can be over its cap. A sweep could skip runs while the tenant's ingest has a global gap. | M3-25 | Open (retargeted by M3-22: Outage behaviour, not load; listed as the staging outage drill (stop Postgres longer than stale_minutes, check queues drain).) |
| DF-770 | M3 run health | A 'not JSON' log record (a cut-off line) sorts first in logs output | Cosmetic: the cut-off line has an empty 'at', so it is shown before the records it followed. It could take the previous record's time instead. | M3-17 | Open |
| DF-771 | M3 run health | An 'agentic edit' cancelled after a policy change still writes run.policy_changed to the audit log | The audit log can show the change one resume early: no ledger event is written until the run actually continues, and the next resume logs it again. Minor; the ledger stays accurate. | M3-17 | Open |
| DF-772 | M3 run health review | Review F3: the policy-change notice and the run.policy_changed audit record are written before the gate decision or resume is known to succeed | A refused approve (no role, four-eyes) prints 'run X continues on policy Y' and writes run.policy_changed, although nothing continues and nothing is stamped. Every retry writes the audit line again. The ledger stays accurate. This covers the developer's deferred 'agentic edit cancelled' item too. Fix: emit both when StampingRecorder consumes the stamp. | M3-17 | Open |
| DF-773 | M3 run health review | Review F4: leaving the heartbeat context can hold the prompt for about 10 s when the control plane is slow or unreachable | __exit__ joins for up to 6 s, then the final waiting beat posts synchronously (3-5 s timeout, 429 retries inside _send). If the join times out, a late 'running' beat can land after the 'waiting' one. Fix: a shorter best-effort timeout and no 429 retry for heartbeats, and the thread must not send another running beat once stop is set. | M3-17 | Open (retargeted by M3-22: CLI heartbeat exit timing; not affected by server load (heartbeat p99 1.04 s at 1000 runs).) |
| DF-774 | M3 run health review | Review F5: the beat interval comes from the CLI's cached org policy, while the zombie window comes from the control plane's active policy | One policy commit that raises heartbeat_seconds and lowers stale_minutes can leave CLIs on the old cache with fewer than 3 beats per window for up to 24 h, so their runs are wrongly marked stale. Fix: return the interval in the heartbeat response, or document the lag. | M3-17 | Open (retargeted by M3-22: Beat interval versus zombie window source is a protocol/CLI change, not load.) |

| DF-779 | M2-58 parity | Zyroflo does not read text that a program other than echo/printf/Write-Output passes into a shell | At 96b3e53 Zyroflo does not read text that cat, head, tr or any program but a printer passes into a shell (cat <<< 'rm -rf x' / sh, cat <(echo rm -rf x) / sh), nor a pipe into a shell past a line break, a comment, a group or a compound (cat <<< 'rm -rf x' /<newline> sh, ... / (cd /tmp; sh), ... / if true; then sh; fi), into tee >(sh), source /dev/stdin, csh, nor a here-string or process substitution reaching a shell as its script operand (bash /dev/stdin <<< ..., bash <(cat <<< ...)), nor at now <<< .... A real shell runs each. agentic refuses them through its own spellings loop, kept for that reason; test_shapes_only_agentics_own_spellings_refuse pins them. Still unread by both: echo 'rm -rf x' / (sh). | Zyroflo PR Z10, then drop agentic's spellings loop (M2-58) | Open |
| DF-780 | M2-58 parity | Zyroflo does not read a brace group in the text an interpreter is told to run | At 96b3e53 Zyroflo reads { rm -rf x; } on its own line but not inside the text an interpreter or carrier runs: bash -c "{ rm -rf x; }", watch "{ rm -rf x; }", 2>&1 bash -c '{ rm -rf x; }', 2>&1 sh -c 'r''m -r x', unshare sh -c '...', ssh host '{ rm -rf x; }', and carriers such as kubectl exec, tmux, xterm -e, script -qc, runuser -c, flock -c. A real shell runs each. agentic refuses them through its own spellings loop, kept for that reason (tests in test_guard_shell_stdin.py). | Zyroflo PR Z10, then drop agentic's spellings loop (M2-58) | Open |
| DF-781 | M2-58 parity | The deny reader's reach heuristics over-read a few shapes, and _scripts inherits them | _carried reads any -...c... word as a shell's -c (bash -Recurse 'rm -rf x'), an unknown watch option is skipped as a flag (watch --hard 'rm -rf x'), and env's three-word reach makes a later watch a command (env /q watch 'rm -rf x'). 6 of the 181 new parity refusals, all random-atom strings that run nothing harmful; they were already the reader's behaviour for deny rules. | guard-semantics row (with DF-346/DF-359) | Open (M2-58: the _scripts part no longer applies, because the variant removal was reverted; the deny reader's _carried, watch-option and env-reach heuristics still over-read these shapes.) |
| DF-782 | M2-58 review | Zyroflo's spellings keep the quotes around a quoted part with spaces, so a GIT_* or PATH/LD_*/BASH_ENV assignment after a separator inside a command string is not seen | bash -c 'ls; GIT_SSH_COMMAND="touch MARK #" git ls-remote ...' makes git run the injected command (shown with a real bash and git), and cmd /c "set GIT_SSH_COMMAND=x && git fetch" and watch 'ls; env PATH=/tmp ls' are the same class. agentic's own quote-stripped spelling refuses them; test_guard_shell_stdin.py pins them. | Zyroflo PR Z10, then drop agentic's spellings loop (M2-58) | Open |
| DF-783 | M2-58 review | A deeply nested command string raises RecursionError in Zyroflo's reader instead of a verdict | ("bash -c '" * 2000)[:16384] raises out of Guard.command, on the old pin too. | DF-791 | M3 ROI review | F4 - chargeback CLI checks its output location only after the export | A missing parent folder fails with exit 1 and a raw FileNotFoundError naming the temp '.part' file. By then the server has already audited the export. The default file name is built from the raw --team. The server validates --team as a slug, so no path traversal is reachable today. The existence check and os.replace leave a TOCTOU window: a file that appears in between is replaced. Fix: check the parent dir before the request (CliError REFUSED with a hint), build the name from the server's report.team, and finish with an exclusive create or link. | M3-01 follow-up (CLI polish) or M3-18 docs/UX row | Open |
| DF-792 | M3 ROI review | F5 - chargeback CSV has no spreadsheet formula-cell guard | Text cells (team, month) are written as they are. Fix: reuse the audit export's leading-quote guard in agentic.orgapi.cost._cell, so server and CLI stay byte-identical, and add a test. | M3-01 follow-up (with the FOCUS mapping item) or ST-1 gateway team validation | Open |

Fix: CAST(NULL AS bigint), then recheck driver_sql and _ROI_MONTH. | M3-01 follow-up | Open |

| DF-837 | M3 load test review | F6: staging mode seeds tenants, 1000 users and 30-day tokens into any DSN and has no cleanup | Nothing stops LT_OWNER_DSN from pointing at production, and the seeded rows and live token hashes stay behind. Add a cleanup command by slug prefix (or short token expiry in staging mode), a confirmation flag for External mode, and a README line saying never to use it on production. | M3-25 | Open |
| DF-838 | M3 load test review | F7: .state/stack.json can enter the image build context | `up` writes the generated passwords before building with the repository root as context, and .dockerignore does not exclude control-plane/loadtest/.state. They land in a build-stage layer of the local builder cache (the final image is clean). Fix: add **/.state to .dockerignore. | M3-25 | Open |
| DF-839 | M3 load test review | F8: replica discovery matches '-lt-cp-' in container names, which fails on Podman | podman-compose names containers with underscores, so bases() finds no replicas. Count replicas with `compose ps --format {{.Service}}`, or document that only Docker was verified. | M3-25 | Open |
| DF-840 | M3 load test review | F9: the 1000-run reference never closed or re-registered a run | Steady-state registration churn at 1000 open runs was not measured at the reference event rate, and the stress run changes both rates at once. Calling the timing 'pessimistic' holds only for the event rate. Do a run of 1800 s or more before calling 3 replicas sufficient, and log the exception text of the single transport error in the Recorder. | M3-25 | Open |
| DF-841 | M3 load test review | F10: kill-poll and heartbeat targets say '< 2 s' in the docs but are compared with <= | The boundary is ambiguous in the target the owner will decide on. Align the docs on '<=' or compare strictly. | M3-25 | Open |
| DF-842 | M3 load test review | F11: plan test paths are wrong; DF-607 and DF-621 bookkeeping | The plan names control-plane/tests/test_loadtest_*.py, but the tests are in control-plane/tests/loadtest/. Change the DF-607 retarget reason to 'the index needs production-sized data to validate', or add the (tenant_id, at COLLATE "C") index test-first. Coordinator: record DF-621 as Retarget (M3-25), not Done, because both of its M3-22 parts were retargeted. | M3-25 | Open |
| DF-843 | M2-61 commit command | In-place components in a run of several components | A run that mixes in-place and tree components, or a monorepo profile with several in-place components in one checkout, is still refused at prepare with a precise reason. Only a run of one in-place component works. | new SP6 worktree row (M2/M3) | Open |
| DF-844 | M2-61 commit command | Chain override commands into the hash-chained run ledger | Each override command is recorded in the run folder (vcs-commands.jsonl, beside events.jsonl). That record is not hash-chained: Zyroflo's ledger takes only its own event types, so chaining it needs a Zyroflo event type (a Zyroflo PR) or a declared ext field on an existing event. | Zyroflo PR (M2 Zyroflo adapters) | Open |
| DF-845 | M2-61 commit command | Checkpoint exclusions and the nested-repository check do not apply in place | GENERATED and org checkpoint_exclude are git pathspecs the framework cannot pass to a repo's own tool, so the tool decides what it commits. Caches may land in the commit unless the repo ignores them. | M2-61 follow-up / SP6 | Open |
| DF-846 | M2-61 commit command | changed() and diff() see only the primary git of an in-place checkout | In a repo with a second git dir (the open-core split case), files tracked only by the second git are missed by the post-step protected-path check (M2-60) and by the review diff. The guard's file-tool checks still apply. | SP6 worktree row | Open |
| DF-847 | M2-61 commit command | Override commands run outside the OS sandbox | Like the framework's own checkpoint commits, they run on the host. The controls are the org allow list, the guard, the changed-script check and stripping AGENTIC_* credentials. Running them inside srt with .git writable is a possible hardening. | M3 sandbox hardening | Open |
| DF-848 | M2-61 commit command review | F5: refusal for a changed commit/rollback script gives no next step | When an agent edits the override's script, every later rollback and fresh begin is refused (fail-closed, which is correct), but the message does not say what to do next. The developer has to work out that they should review the change, discard it by hand (git -C <checkout> checkout -- <file>) and resume. | M2-61 follow-up | Open |
| DF-849 | M2-61 commit command review | F6: commit_override.allow is a word-prefix match | A repository can add any arguments after an allowed prefix (e.g. `monotool commit --exec ...`), and then only the guard's deny lists apply. The template comment now says to list the narrowest command with its flags. An exact-match rule form for orgs that want it is not built yet. | M2-61 follow-up (org policy) | Open |
| DF-850 | M2-61 commit command review | F7: override command record is not hash-chained | vcs-commands.jsonl in the run folder is neither tamper-evident nor exported to the control-plane audit chain until Zyroflo gets an event type for it. Already disclosed as a developer deferral. Do not describe the audit as chained. | Zyroflo PR (M2 Zyroflo adapters) | Open |
| DF-851 | M2-61 commit command review | F8: on a timeout only the direct child process is killed | On Windows, a wrapper such as npm.cmd can leave child processes still writing in the developer's checkout after the framework reports a timeout. The tool's error tail is scrubbed only for credentials in URLs. Fix: kill the whole process tree (taskkill /T on Windows, a process group and killpg on POSIX) and run the framework's secret redactor on the tail. | M3 sandbox hardening | Open |
| DF-852 | M3-19 | `agentic policy check` does not flag a team without `monthly_budget_usd` | The template gives every team a budget and the README says every team sets one, but a team file without one is still valid: that team's gateway spend is capped only per run and by concurrency. | M3-18 (admin guide) or a policy-check warning before GA | Open |
| DF-853 | M3-24 | The public eval corpus is smaller than S10's opt-in plan sizes | Holdout has 20 BA, 20 Design and 20 dev tasks, but S10's non-inferiority plans need 30 (rubric) and 40 (Developer); a plan with n above the holdout size is refused. The pilot's cases must fill the gap: the owner ruled on 2026-10-30 that the first live A/B waits for at least 30 holdout cases per agent (40 for the Developer) and never runs with 20. There is no `agentic eval harvest` yet (S10's harness sketch names it, over Zyroflo corpus_harvest), so cases are added by hand. | M3-25 (with DF-854) | Open |
| DF-854 | M3-24 | First live optimisation opt-in A/B record | The procedure and the record template are proven only on recorded cassettes (docs/runbooks/optimisation-opt-in/dry-run-record.md). The first live record (one group, on the BA and the Developer) needs real models, which are not allowed before M3-25. Depends on DF-853: it runs only once the corpus holds 30 holdout cases per agent (40 for the Developer), owner 2026-10-30. The group order is owner-approved. | M3-25, after DF-853 | Open |
| DF-855 | M3-24 | `agentic eval ab --plan` with a gateway per variant | `run_ab` takes a runner per variant, but the CLI wires only `eval run` and `skills eval` (one gateway). The opt-in A/B needs each variant's runs sent to its own gateway (A: current profile, B: plus the group's overlay). Without it the live record needs a small Python driver. | M3-25 (with DF-854) | Open |
| DF-856 | M3-24 review | The A/B result lacks S10's verbosity check and the judge-family flag | `ABResult` carries no verbosity correlation (S10 flags abs(r) above 0.5 between output length and score) and no judge-same-family flag, so the opt-in record writes 'Verbosity check: not recorded'. EvalReport computes verbosity for single runs only. | M3-25 (with DF-854) | Open |
| DF-857 | M3-19 owner follow-up | Nothing enforces that a team lead may only lower their team's `monthly_budget_usd` | The owner's rule (2026-10-30): org admins change any team's budget, a lead only lowers their own, both by policy-repo PR. CODEOWNERS lists the leads and the org admins for policy/teams/<team>.yaml, and either may approve, so a lead could approve their own raise. Fix: a policy-check (or control-plane sync) rule that a raise must be approved by an org admin, or an org ceiling per team. | M3-18 / before GA | Open |
| DF-890 | M3-04 environments | Scheduled restore drill in the cluster (daily in staging): an image with pg_restore and scripts/restore_drill.py, and a scratch Postgres it may use | The backup runs nightly, but the drill that proves it restorable is still run by hand | M3-05 | Open |
| DF-891 | M3-04 environments | The backup role (BYPASSRLS + pg_read_all_data) is made by hand-written SQL; add `python -m agentic_cp db backup-role` like `db dashboard-role` | An operator who skips the SQL gets a failing backup CronJob (the owner cannot dump: forced RLS) | M3-05 | Open |
| DF-892 | M3-04 environments | Operator commands have no chart-provided pod: an operator must copy AGENTIC_CP_POLICY_BRANCH and both DSNs by hand (kind uses deploy/helm/kind/promotion.sh) | An operator pod without the branch is unpinned for tenant create (sync then refuses that tenant, so nothing wrong is applied, but the error comes late) | M3-05 | Open |
| DF-893 | M3-04 environments | kubeconform skips ExternalSecret and Certificate (CRDs): validate them against pinned CRD schemas | A field-name mistake in those two templates shows only at install time | M3-25 | Open |
| DF-910 | M3-06 retention | Hand the gateway its retention without a copy step | TokenLean has no admin API for retention, so `python -m agentic_cp retention gateway-config --tenant S` prints its `retention:` block and an operator puts it in the gateway's config. A gateway shared by several tenants needs the longest period, chosen by hand, and a change to retention.yaml needs the block regenerated. | TokenLean admin API (ST row) | Open |
| DF-911 | M3-06 retention | Legal holds outside the control plane are records only | Holds on traces, backups, logs outside the sent-mail log and laptops' run folders are not enforced by code: `agentic gc` does not read holds, and the trace store's, backup tool's and log stack's expiry are set by hand (runbook). | E4-01 / E4-02 | Open |
| DF-912 | M3-06 retention | Legal holds on one person, and per-user erasure | Holds name teams and kinds only. A hold on one custodian, and erasing one person's rows (runs they started, their feedback, their email in audit records), are not built. | E4-02 (per-user erasure) | Open |
| DF-913 | M3-06 retention | No retention period for personal skill and memory backups | Decision 115 sets none for personal_events, so they stay insert-only and are kept (see DF-656, DF-666). | OR-01 follow-up | Open |
| DF-914 | M3-06 retention | Trace capture beyond the native backend, and the trace store's period | Only the native backend honours a team's trace_capture; agent-CLI backends never put prompt text in the framework's traces. The 30-day traces period is set in the trace store (Langfuse, the collector's backend) by hand. | E2 telemetry | Open |
| DF-915 | M3-06 retention | External anchor for the audit chain and its retention checkpoints | The checkpoint is inside the hash chain, so it cannot be edited unnoticed, but someone with the owner role could rewrite the whole chain consistently. Exports' end hashes are the only external anchor today; a periodic signed or RFC 3161-timestamped head would close it. | E4-01 | Open |
| DF-916 | M3-06 retention review | Learning proposals keep the ids of pruned feedback | After feedback is pruned, a proposal's evidence lists ids that no longer resolve; the API and the CLI should say they were removed by retention. | M3 learning follow-up | Open |
| DF-917 | M3-06 retention review | A lock-held retention run fails the CronJob's Job | A manual `retention run` overlapping the scheduled one makes the scheduled run exit 4, so its Job fails and retries once: noisy, nothing is lost. | M3 ops | Open |
| DF-918 | M3-06 retention | A deleted tenant's leftovers outside the control-plane database | The Grafana reader role, the policy work folder and the gateway's own records of the tenant stay until an operator removes them (runbook steps). | M3 ops | Open |
